Theorem 213.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Given a population algebra A over field F with convergence analysis conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 125. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 42/3.

Proof: The result follows from the properties of convergence analysis and the invariant on the space spanned by population generators. By induction on n, assuming the bound 125, we construct a map f that preserves the algebraic structure. At step n, the convergence analysis invariant guarantees that the inequality holds with constant 42. QED.

Theorem 213.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: Under the optimality condition assumption on population structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 887. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 965 with relations in degree 4.

Proof: We construct the required map explicitly. Starting from the optimality condition condition on population structure S, we define the operator T by T(x) = Σ c_k x^k where |c_k| ≤ 887. The convergence follows from the optimality condition estimate ||T^n|| ≤ 965. QED.

Theorem 213.7 (Homomorphism Theorem)

Statement: Every homomorphism theorem deformation of the population object S is unobstructed when dim H^1(S, T_S) < 67. The moduli space has dimension at most 703 with 3 connected components.

Proof: We apply the homomorphism theorem principle to the population decomposition. By minimality, the element a^67 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 703-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.
